# docs/MEASUREMENTS.md — the numeric ledger

**Every number, observation and claim this project makes lives here.** Append-only, newest
section last within each table. This project is **not** on a research-publication pathway
(maintainer, 2026-09-23), so there is no paper to be the durable home for a figure — this file is.

Rules:

- A number quoted in `LOG.md`, a handover, a commit message or a chat reply **must** have a row
  here, with the command that reproduces it and the run id that produced it. A figure without a
  row does not count as recorded.
- Rows are never edited or deleted. A superseded value gets a **new row** with a new date; the old
  row stays, so the trajectory is readable.
- "Observation" rows are as welcome as measured ones — a finding that turned out to be an artefact
  is exactly the thing that gets rediscovered expensively later.
- Raw artefacts (JSONL, JSON, HTML audit copies) stay where they are written; this file points at
  them. See §5.

Related: `docs/PAPERS.md` (per-paper Applied/Reference status), `docs/REFERENCES.md`
(machine-verified citation metadata), `docs/handover/PHASE_*.md` (the narrative around a phase).

---

## 1. Extraction and evaluation quality

Reproduced by: `wiki audit eval -s spice-and-wolf` (zero-cost — local/NLI only, completes with
every API key unset; `docs/vision/PHASE_27.md`).

| Date | Metric | Value | Command / run | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2026-09-21 | mean gold fact recall | 39.8% | `wiki audit eval -s spice-and-wolf` | Pre-Vertex baseline. |
| 2026-09-23 | mean gold fact recall | 43.9% | same | After the Vertex flip + four defect fixes (`docs/handover/PHASE_28.md`). |
| 2026-09-23 | mean gold fact recall | **45.9%** | same | Clean protocol re-run from a fresh gazetteer, extraction temperature pinned 0.1. Current. |
| 2026-09-23 | fact precision, all ten gold characters | 1.0 | same | Unchanged across every run above. |
| 2026-09-23 | fact-recall WARNs | 3 of 10 | same | `enek` 25%, `kraft-lawrence` 44%, `yarei` 0%. Left visible on purpose; causes in `docs/handover/PHASE_28.md` §8. |
| 2026-09-23 | citation entailment, `history` | grounded (492 cited paragraphs) | same | `appearance` / `personality` read "weak overlap" on 1 and 3 cited paragraphs — a sample-size artefact, not a grounding failure. |
| 2026-09-23 | claims, v01 / v02 | 166+20 / 270+20 | `wiki audit claims -s spice-and-wolf` | Window pass + scene pass. Was 215 / 411 on 2026-09-21. |
| 2026-09-23 | verify flags raised | 14 across 10 of 14 characters | `wiki verify -s spice-and-wolf --upto 2` | Was 0 — `verify` had been failing open (`{"bad_facts": [...]}` validating against `flags: []`). |

**Observation (2026-09-23) — floors are not ratcheted onto a single run.** A single entity's
recall carries about ±1 fact of noise at this corpus size, so a floor in
`docs/eval/gold/spice-and-wolf/baseline.json` is raised only from a run reproduced twice. The
file's own `_comment` says the same thing.

**Observation (2026-09-23) — `yarei` 0% recall is correct behaviour, not a regression.** The gold
fact is "held in the barn for a week"; the corpus sentence is conditional ("Yarei *would be*
locked in a granary … close to a week"). An extractor that declines to assert a hypothetical is
right and gold is wrong. Fix gold, then re-record the floor — do not tune the floor to silence
the WARN.

| 2026-09-23 | character pages carrying a portrait | 11 of 14 | same | Was 14 of 14, but the v01 frontispiece was the "portrait" on Holo's, Lawrence's **and** Yarei's pages at once. See the observation below. |

| 2026-09-23 | pytest | 1028 passed | `pytest` | |

**Observation (2026-09-23) — nothing in this pipeline can see what an art plate depicts.**
`cover_image_for_entity` answers only "which plate is nearest this character's debut", which
collides by construction: the v01 frontispiece is nearest the debut of Holo, Lawrence and Yarei
alike, and the emitter captioned it with each of their names in turn. The fix is a cap
(front-matter plates only for the two leads; any plate on at most two pages), not a better guess.
A vision model reading the plates is the only thing that would actually know.

**Observation (2026-09-23) — the codex `related` bucket was information-free on this corpus.**
It is the entry's personalized-PageRank neighbours, and on a 2-volume graph every place and
faction alike listed the same four globally-central entities (Medio / Milone / Remelio / Rowen).
That is a property of PPR at this graph size, not a fact about any entry, so it is no longer
rendered. It is still computed, still in the bundle and still in `docs/CONTRACTS.md` §6.1 —
re-render it if a larger graph makes the neighbour sets actually differ per entry.

**Observation (2026-09-23) — `members` and `adversaries` were not mutually exclusive.** Milone
Company rendered "Members: Kraft Lawrence, Marheit" directly above "Adversaries: Kraft Lawrence,
Yarei"; Remelio listed Norah Arendt in both. Each came from a real pair of intervals (an employee
who is also betrayed by the company), but a reader sees a contradiction, not a nuance. Membership
now wins; the betrayal is still told in prose and on the relationship page.

## 4. Known gaps carrying a number

| Date opened | Gap | Number | Where it is tracked |
|---|---|---|---|
| 2026-09-23 | Character-page length imbalance | 21.4× between the shortest and longest page | §3 above. A fandom wiki's stub-vs-feature spread is real but nothing like this; the short pages are evidence-starved, not capped. |
| 2026-09-23 | Scene-pass fact yield is low on `vertex_flash` | ~60–80 raw `participant_facts` per volume, ~20 surviving the gates | `docs/handover/PHASE_28.md` §8. The window pass carries claim recall today. |
| 2026-09-21 | `docs/REFERENCES.md` is stale | 51 rows against 54 PDFs | `docs/handover/PHASE_28.md` §8. arXiv returns 200 for an already-served API URL and 406 for every uncached one — server-side refusal, not a bad id. `docs/PAPERS.md` is complete at 54/54. Re-run `python docs/fetch_reference_pdfs.py --check` on a day arXiv answers. |
| 2026-09-23 | `synthesize` and `verify` are still serial | ~2.5 min for two volumes | `docs/handover/PHASE_28.md` §8. Both share one SQLite connection across the loop. Not urgent. |
| 2026-09-23 | Gold covers one series | 10 characters, Spice and Wolf v1–2 | `docs/eval/gold/spice-and-wolf/`. |
| 2026-09-23 | `docs/REFERENCES.md` regeneration attempted again | 0 of 58 ids resolved | `python docs/fetch_reference_pdfs.py --check` | Every id failed, not just the uncached ones — see the observation below. Script wrote nothing (correct on a partial fetch); `git status` clean afterwards. |

**Observation (2026-09-23) — the arXiv diagnosis in `docs/handover/PHASE_28.md` §8 needs
refining.** That entry says arXiv returns 200 for an API URL it has already served and 406 for
every uncached one. Today's run contradicts the first half: **all 58 tracked ids failed**,
including ones the script had resolved successfully before. So the failure is not selective
caching behaviour — on a bad day arXiv is simply unreachable from here for the whole batch. The
script's "write nothing on a partial fetch" behaviour is what keeps this from corrupting the file,
and it is the reason the gap is still open rather than half-closed.

**Observation (2026-09-23) — three separate counts, all different.** 58 arXiv ids tracked by the
script, 54 PDFs in `docs/Sample_Research_Related/`, 51 rows in `docs/REFERENCES.md`. The 58-vs-54
gap is ids cited without a PDF kept locally; the 54-vs-51 gap is the stale regeneration above.
`docs/PAPERS.md` is keyed on filename and is complete at 54/54. Reconcile all three the day arXiv
answers, and record the reconciled numbers here.

## 6. Where the raw artefacts live

| What | Path |
|---|---|
| Every LLM request/response of a run | `data/<series>/_runs/<run_id>/calls.jsonl` |
| Content-addressed prompt cache (shared across series) | `data/cache/llm/` |
| Per-stage drop counters | `data/<series>/_runs/<run_id>/yield.json` |
| Saved HTML audit reports | `data/<series>/_runs/<run_id>/audit/<stage>.html` |
| Gold data and recall floors | `docs/eval/gold/<series>/` (`baseline.json` holds the floors) |
| Probe measurements, one JSON object per line | `docs/probe/*.jsonl` |
| Stored-prompt replay harnesses (the cheap way to A/B) | `docs/eval/replay_context_budget.py`, `docs/eval/nli_kind_sweep.py`, `docs/probe/gate_comparison_option_a.py` |

## 11. Work package B — making the gated comparison able to answer its own question — 2026-09-23

Still $0: no model call, no artifact regenerated, budget $5.49. Everything here is about the
scoring harness that the one approval-gated `--execute` call will be judged by. Reproduce with
`python docs/eval/probe_sparse_scene.py` (prepare only) and
`python -m pytest tests/test_sparse_scene_probe.py -q`.

| Date | Metric / observation | Value | Command / run | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2026-09-23 | Precision controls fired on the predicate family, not the forbidden value | Inverted: a claim recording Saint Ruvinheigen as **"long dead" scored as a precision violation** | `score_against_fixture([... STATUS "long dead" ...])` before 2026-09-23 fix | R8's whole point is that the page must not call a centuries-dead saint *alive*. Matching any STATUS claim meant the candidate that **fixes** the defect was scored as causing it — a rejected winner. Fixture facts now carry `forbidden_value` and the scorer refuses a `must_not_assert` fact without one, rather than silently falling back. |
| 2026-09-23 | Family-only matching credited the wrong fact and hid the right one | One BACKGROUND claim about the cathedral mural reported "recovered R3" (his battles) while R4 (the mural) stayed unmatched | Same call, `predicate: BACKGROUND, value: "pictured on the cathedral mural"` | Both a false positive and a false negative from a single claim. `recovered` is now `candidate_recovered`, carries the claim's own value beside the reviewed quote and an `adjudicated: null` a human fills in, and requires the claim to cite the paragraph the fact was read from. docs/tasks/0006 always specified a manual decision metric; the harness now matches it. |
| 2026-09-23 | Inbound credit applied to every predicate carrying an object | `ORIGIN_FROM pasloe` (family GOVERNS) registered as a RELATIONSHIP of Pasloe | Same call | Harmless only because no fixture fact has `pasloe` as subject. Restricted to relationship-family predicates. |
| 2026-09-23 | **B1's scoreable ceiling before the above fixes** | **2 of 7** dev `missing` facts | `_PREDICATE_TO_FAMILY` values vs. fixture families | M3 INFLUENCE, M4 REPUTATION, R4 DEPICTION, R5 NAMESAKE and E5 PATRONAGE had no predicate that could produce them, so no B1 output could ever be credited with them. The gated call would have returned at most 2/7 whatever the model did. |
| 2026-09-23 | B1's scoreable ceiling after | **7 of 7**, as human-adjudicated candidates | Same comparison including `_NARRATIVE_FAMILIES` | `BACKGROUND` ("a biographical fact, not a narrative", 20 words) is the only thing in the vocabulary that can carry those five, so it now stands as a candidate for every narrative family instead of `HISTORY` alone. The reviewer decides; nothing is auto-credited. |
| 2026-09-23 | Historical baseline, rescored under the corrected harness | **0 of 7 recovered, 0 precision violations** — unchanged | `python docs/eval/probe_sparse_scene.py` | The stored response contains no saint `participant_facts` at all, so the conclusion never depended on the scoring defects. The numbers now mean what they say. |
| 2026-09-23 | Test suite | **1047 passed** | `python -m pytest -q` | +4: the inverted control, the refusal to score a `must_not_assert` fact with no forbidden value, paragraph-citation for candidates, and relationship-only inbound credit. One of them caught a duplicate-candidate bug in the narrative-family change as it was written. |

The Overview line renders it honestly as "alive _(assumed)_"; the lead is a bare scan line with nowhere to put that marker, so it asserted it. Both saints led with "alive" — one of them a man Holo bit centuries before the story begins. |
| 2026-09-23 | Leads after the fix | **6 evidenced statuses kept, 8 unevidenced dropped**; 4 pages now have no lead strip | `wiki synthesize -s spice-and-wolf --upto 2 --force` then `wiki site build ... --force` | Kept: Lawrence "captured", Zheren "presumed alive", Holo, Liebert, Norah, Yarei. Dropped for Enek, both saints, Weiz, Ehrendott, Remelio, Jakob, Marheit. The rule is `inferred`, not STATUS and not a series: any attribute with a configured default is skipped in the lead, and a series that wants different behaviour sets it in its own extraction overlay (`paths.extraction_overlay_config`, already deep-merged). |
| 2026-09-23 | Page-length spread after the fix | 21.4× → **21.7×** (69–1,497 words); cutoff 1 5.60× → **5.65×** | `python docs/eval/character_evidence_coverage.py --cutoff 2` | The ratio got *worse* by one word per stub, because removing a false claim shortens the shortest pages. Recorded precisely because it is the clearest case yet that page-length ratio is not the objective — `docs/handover/PHASE_29.md` §7 and `docs/tasks/0006` both say so, and here honesty and the ratio point in opposite directions. |
| 2026-09-23 | Audits after the change | `pages` OK, `links` OK, `relationships` OK, `eval` OK with the same 3 known recall WARNs (Enek 25%, Lawrence 44%, Yarei 0%) | `wiki audit <stage> -s spice-and-wolf` | No fact-precision regression, no negative-fact violation, 86 pages unchanged. |
| 2026-09-23 | Diagnostic hard-coded to one series | `character_evidence_coverage.py` pinned `"spice-and-wolf"` in 4 places and refused any cutoff outside `(1, 2)` | Now `--series`/`-s`, cutoff defaults to the highest ingested volume, output `character_evidence_coverage_<series>_v<NN>.json` | The `(1, 2)` guard was a spend guard copied into a script that makes no model calls, so all it did was stop the diagnostic being pointed at the next series. Scope now comes from which volumes are parsed on disk. New `paths.active_series()` is the read side of `set_active_series`. |
| 2026-09-23 | Scorer silently dropped predicates its map had not heard of | Any predicate outside `_PREDICATE_TO_FAMILY` was ignored | `score_against_fixture(...)["unmapped_predicates"]` | The map copies a vocabulary that lives in `config/extraction.yaml`, which every series may extend through its overlay, so a new predicate would quietly stop counting. It now falls back to the predicate's own name as the family and reports what was unmapped. |
| 2026-09-23 | Test suite | **1049 passed** | `python -m pytest -q` | +2: the lead never stating an inferred default, and an unmapped predicate being reported rather than dropped. |

## 16. Canonicalization threshold: a negative result worth not rediscovering - 2026-09-23

$0, local bge-m3 only (CLAUDE.md SS2 permits free local models for diagnosis). Nothing rebuilt.

| Date | Metric / observation | Value | Command / run | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2026-09-23 | Canonicalization did run on the shipped graph | **23 `canonicalize` calls** in the last graph build | `data/spice-and-wolf/_runs/20260922T212939-graph_build-v1-2/calls.jsonl` | Ruled out the obvious explanation first: the near-duplicate skills are not the result of an unreachable embed model no-opping the stage. bge-m3 is pulled and reachable; the duplicates survived `similarity_threshold: 0.86`. |
| 2026-09-23 | Pairwise bge-m3 cosine over Holo's 17 SKILL values | `detecting fraud`/`detecting lies` **0.854**; `discerning coin purity by sound`/`can distinguish coins by scent` **0.770**; `can sense lies`/`detecting lies` **0.756**; `predicting weather`/`detecting lies` **0.641**; `witty`/`extraordinary wits` **0.628** | Embed each value via `http://localhost:11434/api/embed` (bge-m3), cosine over all pairs | **Similarity does not rank by duplication on this data.** The genuine duplicate `can sense lies`/`detecting lies` (0.756) scores *below* two genuinely distinct skills - coin purity **by sound** vs distinguishing coins **by scent** (0.770), which a merge would destroy. The other genuine duplicate, `witty`/`extraordinary wits` (0.628), scores below the unrelated pair `predicting weather`/`detecting lies` (0.641). |
| 2026-09-23 | Decision: `similarity_threshold` stays at **0.86** | Unchanged | - | No threshold separates duplicates from distinct values here. Lowering it to catch 0.756 first merges the sound/scent pair at 0.770, and still misses 0.628. Recorded so this is not re-tuned later in hope of a win. A real fix is per-group adjudication, not a number; it would bill, and the residue is cosmetic redundancy on one section of one page. |

| 2026-09-23 | Why `places` showed none of them | `max_key_events` was unset for the kind, AND `_faction_key_events` returns `[]` for a roster under 2 | `config/extraction.yaml` `codex_pages`; `site/bundle.py::_faction_key_events` | Two independent blocks. ## 22. Structured-wiki architecture review — 2026-09-24

Review/run ID: `20260924-structured-wiki-review`; code baseline
`0a9794e1ea8c4c43b64fd0b801a9281e16106b19`. Reproduction **R**:
`python docs/research/2026-09-24-structured-wiki/audit_snapshot.py`.
The [snapshot](research/2026-09-24-structured-wiki/snapshot.json) hashes its inputs;
live DB reads use SQLite `mode=ro`. Synthetic witnesses are not corpus prevalence estimates.
The [vision](vision/2026-09-24-structured-wiki-quality.md) and
[implementation plan](tasks/0007-structured-wiki-quality.md) interpret these findings.

## 28. A directional reversal, and verification verdicts that do not hold still — 2026-09-24

Total spend for everything in this section: **$0.0600** (lifetime $5.4889 → $5.5489 of the $20
cap). `graph build` $0.0539, `synthesize` + `site build` $0.0061.

| Date | Observation / metric | Value | Exact command / run | Interpretation |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2026-09-24 | Directional predicates exempted from the reversal check | 2 of 2 (`SAVED`, `KILLED`) | `_relation_conflict_groups(settings.relations)` vs each predicate's `symmetric` flag | `_strip_mutual_direction_collisions` skipped any predicate in a `conflicts_with` group, on the recorded assumption that every grouped predicate is symmetric. SAVED and KILLED declare each other and are both directional, so they were exempt from the only check that catches a same-predicate reversal — and the grouped path cannot substitute, since it buckets by the already-canonicalized pair and never sees a raw reversal. |
| 2026-09-24 | Live consequence | 1 relation: `kraft-lawrence SAVED holo`, qualifier "Holo stopped the wolves from attacking Lawrence" | `SELECT subject,predicate,object,qualifier FROM intervals WHERE predicate='SAVED'` | Extracted from the same sentence as the correct direction and rendered under a heading saying Lawrence saved Holo — the row's own qualifier contradicted its subject. Gone after the fix; the correct direction survives and the drop is logged as `rule:mutual_direction_collision` / `extraction_error`, not silently discarded. |
| 2026-09-24 | Tiebreak before the fix | Both directions at confidence 1.0; winner decided by dict iteration order | `_rank_key` returns `claim_id` as tiebreak only when `_support_score` is present, else `""` | A coin flip on which direction survives is worse than not checking, so the check alone would not have been a fix. Direction strength now ranks whole side against whole side by (max confidence, distinct evidence count, lowest claim_id): 2 distinct quotes for the correct direction vs 1 for the reversal. Order-independence is asserted in `tests/test_graph.py`. |
| 2026-09-24 | `b4b04c6`'s claim-id split reaching the live graph | claims 446 → 460 (+14); 9 of 9 hand-reviewed fixture pointers went stale | `python -m pytest tests/test_character_evidence_coverage.py` | The first rebuild after polarity/qualifier joined `make_claim_id`. `tests/test_character_evidence_coverage.py::test_covered_facts_still_point_at_claims_that_exist` caught it. Each stale id resolved to exactly one claim with identical subject/predicate/target/first_vol/polarity/qualifier, so the fixture pointers were re-pointed deterministically; no reviewed judgement changed (`claim_id_migrations` in the fixture). |
| 2026-09-24 | **Verification verdicts are not stable across a rebuild** | 12 intervals newly `unsupported`, 1 no longer; unsupported 14 → 25 of ~350 | `SELECT ... FROM verdicts JOIN intervals` on `graph.db` before vs after | The rebuild changed exactly **one** relation, yet re-rolled 12 reader-visible gates. 4 of 5 sampled flips had an identical `interval_id` **and** identical evidence count — the same fact with the same cited quotes, judged the opposite way. |
| 2026-09-24 | Why they move: verification batches per character | 1 LLM call per character, facts sent as a numbered list, flags returned as `fact_index` | `graph/verify.py` module docstring; `_user_prompt`; `verify_character` | Two consequences, both structural. (1) Any change to one of a character's facts changes the whole prompt, so every other verdict for that character is re-rolled — 4 of the 5 sampled flips are Kraft Lawrence's, whose fact set moved. (2) Judgements bleed between facts in one call: `norah-arendt COMMANDS enek` was withheld with the rationale "The evidence describes Enek following orders, not Norah commanding **Liebert**" — a blend of two adjacent COMMANDS facts. A shepherdess commanding her sheepdog is true and was withheld on an argument about a different pair. An in-range but wrong `fact_index` withholds the wrong fact with no way to detect it. |
| 2026-09-24 | Reader-visible cost of the re-roll (regression) | Relation rows across relationship pages 34 → 23; heading-only stub pages 6 → 11; character pages with an empty Relationships section 2 → 4 | Count `- ` bullets before `## Shared scenes` in `dist/spice-and-wolf/wiki/*/relationships/*.md`, baseline vs now | **Not caused by the SAVED fix**, which removed exactly one row. Caused by re-verification. Several newly withheld facts are correct to withhold (`yarei RELATIVE_OF zheren`, `marheit AFFILIATED_WITH medio-company` — wrong company, `kraft-lawrence ENEMY_OF milone-company` — Milone is his ally in v1). Others look like false negatives (`norah-arendt COMMANDS enek`, `kraft-lawrence ENEMY_OF remelio-company` — Remelio threatens to kill him in v2). Net against §2b this is worse: 5 more dead-end pages. |
| 2026-09-24 | Unpopulated stability machinery already in the schema | `verdicts.context_sha256`, `evidence_cutoff`, `model_revision`: NULL on all 348 rows | `SELECT count(*) FROM verdicts WHERE context_sha256 IS NULL` | `graph/store.py::reset()` deletes `verdicts` on every `graph build`, so nothing can be carried forward even when a fact's own evidence is untouched. The columns needed to do so exist and are unwritten. This is WP2's exit criterion "identical visible evidence gives stable earlier snapshots", still unmet. |
| 2026-09-24 | Full suite | 1074 passed, exit 0 | `python -m pytest -q -p no:cacheprovider` | Includes the new reversal test, which fails on the pre-fix code with 2 SAVED edges instead of 1. |

## 29. Verdict stability: a rebuild now reproduces the wiki byte for byte — 2026-09-24

The fix for §28's instability finding. **$0.00** — lifetime unchanged at $5.5489 of the $20 cap
across three full `graph build` runs, because no verification call was needed after the first.

| Date | Observation / metric | Value | Exact command / run | Interpretation |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2026-09-24 | Verdict reuse after the change | Rebuild 1 (populating): 14 characters checked, 0 reused. Rebuild 2 and 3: **0 checked, 14 reused, 348 verdicts kept** | `python -m narrativewiki.cli graph build --series spice-and-wolf`, three times in a row | The first rebuild had to re-verify because the stored verdicts predated `context_sha256` (NULL on all 348 rows, §28). From then on a character whose facts and evidence are all unchanged is not re-asked. `store.reset()` no longer wipes `verdicts`; `prune_orphan_verdicts` enforces that a verdict cannot outlive its interval. |
| 2024-09-24 | Unsupported count across rebuilds | 25, 25, 25 (7.2% of 348) | `python -m narrativewiki.cli audit verify --series spice-and-wolf` | Was 14 → 25 on the one rebuild before the fix (§28). Now it does not move at all unless a fact does. |
| 2026-09-24 | **Emitted wiki across a full rebuild round-trip** | Byte-identical, 0 files differ | `diff -rq` a saved `dist/spice-and-wolf/wiki` against the tree after `graph build` + `synthesize --upto 2` + `site build` | WP2's exit criterion "identical visible evidence gives stable earlier snapshots", met for this pipeline. Before the fix the same round-trip cost 11 relation rows and 5 new dead-end pages. |
| 2026-09-24 | Granularity of the reuse unit | Per character, not per fact | `graph/verify.py` makes one call per character | Deliberate, and a limit worth stating: the prompt covers a character's whole numbered fact list, so a subset cannot be re-verified without changing what the model sees. A character with one changed fact still has all of their verdicts re-rolled. That is the remaining half of §28's finding — the cross-fact bleed (`norah-arendt COMMANDS enek` withheld on a rationale about Liebert) needs per-fact calls, which is a separate decision with a real call-count cost. |
| 2026-09-24 | Reporting fidelity side effect | `characters_flagged` 11 → 12; `facts_flagged` unchanged at 25 | `verification.json` summary | Weiz is the addition. `kraft-lawrence FRIEND_OF weiz` is flagged from Lawrence's side, so Weiz's page already withheld it, but the old report only listed a character whose *own* call produced a flag — so Weiz read as "ok" while a fact was being withheld from its page. The reuse path lists every character carrying an unsupported verdict. The audit gate's numerator and denominator are untouched. |
| 2026-09-24 | Full suite | 1077 passed, exit 0 | `python -m pytest -q -p no:cacheprovider` | Adds a parametrized reuse test (unchanged fact → no call and the earlier judgement kept, including its rationale reaching `verification.json`; moved fact → re-verified, stale verdict not honoured) and a context-hash test (stable, evidence-order-independent, not keyed on `interval_id`, sensitive to a changed quote/qualifier/value). |

## 30. Per-fact verification: better on traits, worse on relations — 2026-09-24

Spend for this section: **$0.7992** (lifetime $5.5489 → $6.3481 of the $20 cap). Per-fact verify
$0.1252 · re-extraction $0.5545 (v1 $0.2013, v2 $0.3532) · rebuild verify delta $0.0891 ·
synthesize/site $0.0304.

| Date | Observation / metric | Value | Exact command / run | Interpretation |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2026-09-24 | Per-fact verification cost | 348 calls, $0.1252, concurrency 4 | `python -m narrativewiki.cli verify --series spice-and-wolf --upto 2` | Replaces 14 batched per-character calls. Cheaper than feared: a per-fact prompt is far smaller than one carrying a character's whole fact list (Holo's had 150 facts at up to 20 quotes each), so total tokens barely moved. Removes `fact_index` entirely, so a judgement can no longer land on a fact it was not about. |
| 2026-09-24 | **Verdict flips are systematically split by fact kind** | 16 released, **16 of 16 relations**; 26 newly withheld, **25 of 26 attributes** | Join `verdicts` to `intervals` on the batched vs per-fact `graph.db` | Not noise. Per-fact is sharper on attributes — it can weigh one quote against one trait — and more permissive on relations, because it no longer sees the character's other facts. The batching was not purely harmful: it incidentally supplied cross-fact context that relation judgements were using. |
| 2026-09-24 | Attribute catches gained (correct) | `NICKNAME 'Holo'` (her name, not a nickname — the defect `docs/vision/PHASE_30.md` named on 2026-09-23); `PERSONALITY 'drunk'` (a state, not a trait); `'always-quick'` (describes her eating); `'jealous'` (the quote says it was *not* jealousy); `'softhearted'` (Holo describing *Lawrence*); `SKILL 'can defeat a hundred armed men'` (Lawrence's question, never confirmed) | `data/spice-and-wolf/04_graph/verification.json` | Of 26 new withholdings roughly 18 read as correct on the cited quote, including several a reader would call obvious errors. This is the change's real gain. |
| 2026-09-24 | Attribute catches that are wrong | `norah-arendt STATUS 'alive'`, `zheren STATUS 'alive'` (both are), `saint-ruvinheigen APPEARANCE 'missing left arm'` (gold fixture R2), `kraft-lawrence ENEMY_OF liebert` "man who attempted to kill him" (he did) | Same | ~4 false negatives. Withholding "alive" is a poor failure. Not fixed here; recorded. |
| 2026-09-24 | **Relation releases shipped a false marriage** | `holo SPOUSE_OF kraft-lawrence`, qualifier "false identity for travel", rendered on **6 pages** | `grep -rn "Spouse of" dist/spice-and-wolf/wiki/` | The batched pass caught it ("a false identity used to explain her presence in a church"); the per-fact pass approved it and it reached `holo.md`, `kraft-lawrence.md` and the pair page at both cutoffs. Lawrence introduces Holo as "My wife" to get her into a church. A §2b hard violation — a page asserting what the text does not support. Also wrongly released: `marheit AFFILIATED_WITH medio-company` (the evidence is about *Milone*), `norah-arendt ENEMY_OF ruvinheigen` (a city), `enek ENEMY_OF holo` (a sheepdog). |
| 2026-09-24 | Fixed deterministically, not by prompt | `SPOUSE_OF.qualifier_disqualifiers` = RELATIVE_OF's pretence list + `false identity`, `false name`, `cover story`; 0 occurrences of "Spouse of" in the emitted tree | `config/extraction.yaml`; `grep -ro 'Spouse of' dist/spice-and-wolf/wiki/ \| wc -l` | `SPOUSE_OF`'s own config note already warned about exactly this ("only an EXPLICITLY STATED marriage counts") and `RELATIVE_OF` already had the list; `SPOUSE_OF` simply lacked it. A fixed phrase belongs in a rule, not in a model's judgement — the note alone failed, and verification caught it only under one of two methods. The gate fired `qualifier_disqualifies_predicate` 16 times in v02. |
| 2026-09-24 | **A config edit is not a surgical change** | v01 claims 166 → 183; 2 of 23 gold-fixture facts lost outright | `python -m narrativewiki.cli extract --series spice-and-wolf --volumes 1 --force` | `extract/adequacy.py::prompt_rules()` injects the gate config INTO the extraction prompt, so editing `qualifier_disqualifiers` made every `claim_extract` call a cache miss and re-rolled the claim set rather than merely re-filtering it. Collateral: `saint-metrogius OCCUPATION 'saint of business'` and `saint-ruvinheigen APPEARANCE 'missing left arm'` (both `already_covered` in the hand-reviewed fixture, neither related to SPOUSE_OF) vanished from the graph. Recorded as `lost` in the fixture's `claim_id_migrations`, never re-pointed at a different claim, and their `status` moved to `missing` — the reviewer's judgement about the text is unchanged, the pipeline's coverage is not. **Expect this cost before editing extraction config.** |

Stubs are still above the original 6: 9 pairs have every relation withheld. §2b's "no dead-end stubs" is not met. |

## 46. Retrospective audit and historical numerical provenance — 2026-09-26

Documentation-only reconstruction: [project lifeline](PROJECT_LIFELINE_AUDIT.md) and
[numerical source catalogue](PROJECT_LIFELINE_METRICS.md). No pipeline experiment was rerun,
no new model-quality measurement was made, and no paid model call was made for this audit.
The catalogue incorporates the historical numerical passages from phase designs, handovers,
plans, VISION and LOG by reference into this ledger, alongside a snapshot of §§1–45.
It deliberately retains superseded results and corrections. Numbers embedded in dates,
configuration examples, estimates and proposed experiments are not all empirical results.

The table below registers the main pre-ledger comparisons used in the narrative. **Command/run
provenance is historical:** exact commands and run identifiers are retained in the catalogue
where the original source supplied them; where absent, they remain not recorded. A phase link
is a documentary locator, not an invented run ID. Old broad-corpus commands are archival, not
authorisation to run them now. Later explicit corrections take precedence over earlier prose.
